state without impacting any branches by switching back to a branch.

If you want to create a new branch to retain commits you create, you may
do so (now or later) by using -c with the switch command. Example:

  git switch -c <new-branch-name>

Or undo this operation with:

  git switch -

Turn off this advice by setting config variable advice.detachedHead to false

            "body": "closes #21218\n\nthis PR makes background-task delivery work across highly available Prefect API replicas. Existing single-process deployments keep the in-memory queue; servers using the existing Redis messaging configuration automatically use durable Redis Streams delivery.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Root cause and design</summary>\n\n`.delay()` currently enqueues the new task run in the API process that handles the request. A TaskWorker's websocket is sticky to one API replica, so a worker connected to replica B cannot see work held by replica A, and scaling A down loses that work.\n\nThis change separates task-run publishing from TaskWorker delivery behind a small publisher/consumer contract:\n\n- the existing memory implementation preserves current local behavior and queue backpressure;\n- `prefect_redis` stores scheduled and retry deliveries in Redis Streams, partitioned by task key for TaskWorker routing;\n- Redis consumer groups reserve each delivery for one API replica and reclaim reservations abandoned by a failed replica;\n- acknowledgements remove delivered entries atomically, while graceful disconnects atomically move unacknowledged work to the retry stream;\n- retry streams retain priority over newly scheduled work.\n\nThe backend follows `PREFECT_MESSAGING_BROKER`, so the Redis configuration already documented for HA enables durable task delivery without another server setting or a background-task-specific HA procedure.\n\n</details>\n\n<details>\n<summary>Behavior and compatibility</summary>\n\n- TaskWorkers still execute tasks through the existing websocket protocol.\n- Prefect remains authoritative for task state, orchestration retries, parameter storage, and persisted results; Redis is only the delivery queue.\n- Delivery is at least once across API failure. A crash after reservation but before TaskWorker acknowledgement is reclaimed after the visibility timeout.\n- Orchestration retries create a new high-priority delivery; transport redelivery remains separate from task retry accounting.\n- `TaskQueue` and `MultiQueue` remain importable with their existing process-local behavior.\n- Deployments without Redis continue to use the existing in-memory queue.\n\n</details>\n\n<details>\n<summary>Empirical validation</summary>\n\n- Existing background-task and websocket subscription suites pass unchanged.\n- Redis contract tests cover task-key routing, competing consumers, retry priority, acknowledgement, graceful release, and crash recovery.\n- A two-API-process reproduction submits through replica A, reserves through replica B, sends `SIGKILL` to B before acknowledgement, and observes the same task run reclaimed by A after the configured visibility window.\n- Under the documented service-separated HA topology, 5,000 mapped tasks were submitted through replica A and executed through a TaskWorker connected to replica B. All 5,000 completed, no task failed, and sampled persisted results (`0`, `2500`, and `4999`) were correct.\n\n</details>\n\n<details>\n<summary>Performance</summary>\n\nExact-main and branch runs used the documented `.map(..., deferred=True)` + TaskWorker shape, PostgreSQL, worker concurrency 100, and 5,000 no-op tasks:\n\n| | `main` | this PR | change |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| submission throughput | 38.69 tasks/s | 38.82 tasks/s | +0.31% |\n| end-to-end throughput | 38.65 tasks/s | 38.77 tasks/s | +0.30% |\n| completed tasks | 5,000 | 5,000 | — |\n| sampled results | correct | correct | — |\n\nThe HA run used two API replicas, a separate services process, Redis messaging/Docket, and cross-replica task delivery. It submitted at 37.84 tasks/s and completed all 5,000 tasks end to end at 32.99 tasks/s, including the services process's event-recording tail.\n\n</details>\n",
